    "newString": "DeBERTa-based filtering offers no advantage over 15D OBD on web text (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_filtering}), confirming $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as operationally binding regardless of feature expressiveness.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}\n\\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 7 continuation-mode C4 models (1B--14B) with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\\Kempirical{=}7$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ reflects concentrated information loss: starting from first-generation text ($\\auc_{0v1} \\geq 0.92$), a single additional generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content (1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp; 2v3 falls below $\\theta$).\n\n\\paragraph{Operational value of the precise boundary.}\nKnowing that discrimination ``collapses early'' provides no actionable guidance: it does not distinguish binary from graduated filtering, nor specify at which generation to draw the threshold.\n$\\Kempirical{=}2$ resolves this: for web text, graduated weighting beyond depth~2 offers no advantage over unfiltered baselines (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}), confirming $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as the operationally binding boundary.\nThe $\\theta$-invariant, feature-set-invariant domain ordering arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 $=$ Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) extends this precision across the domain spectrum (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}), making it predictive for unseen corpora within these domain types.\nContamination rate experiments corroborate this: binary advantage scales with contamination severity while graduated alternatives show no improvement (Appendix~\\ref{sec:rate_sweep}).\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\\ retrain-chain.}\nAll main results use prompt-chain (a fixed model recursively generating from its own outputs), so $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects text-intrinsic information loss.\nRetrain-chain experiments (LoRA fine-tuning each generation) yield $\\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, and $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), placing prompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as the curation floor and retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{=}7$ as the protocol-dependent upper bound.\n\n\\paragraph{Wikipedia single-generation saturation.}\nWikipedia's template-driven structure causes generated text to converge rapidly toward the domain distribution, consistent with its low TTR and entropy (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}); the regularity that makes encyclopedic text easy to generate also eliminates depth signal after a single generation.\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay and domain dependence.}\nToken-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.9$), arXiv decays $3\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$), and the retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}); the ordering correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).\n\n\\paragraph{Feature-space ceiling and $\\Kempirical_{\\text{15D}}$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical_{\\text{intrinsic}}$.}\n(a)~The domain ordering (arXiv $>$ C4 $=$ Code $>$ Wikipedia) is \\emph{feature-set invariant}: it holds under both 15D statistics and DeBERTa fine-tuning, making it a text-intrinsic structural property.\n(b)~Absolute $\\Kempirical$ values are \\emph{feature-dependent}: DeBERTa pushes $\\Kempirical$ to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}), confirming that 15D measurements provide a conservative lower bound on $\\Kempirical_{\\text{intrinsic}}$.\n(c)~On C4 and Code, DeBERTa matches the 15D bound ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), establishing a \\emph{representation-independent boundary}: even 184M-parameter contextual embeddings cannot surpass $\\Kempirical{=}2$ on web text; LightGBM 1v2 AUC declines only 0.8\\,pp from 1.5B to 32B (three data points, insufficient for extrapolation).\nThis extends to downstream curation: DeBERTa-based filtering offers no advantage over 15D OBD on web text (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_filtering}), confirming that the $\\Kempirical{=}2$ boundary is operationally binding regardless of feature expressiveness.\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-source invariance and implications.}\nMulti-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains, confirming $\\Kempirical$ as an intrinsic domain property; binary filtering suffices for web text and code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), while academic text ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) may benefit from finer stratification.\n\n\\paragraph{Connection to attractor dynamics.}\nWang et al.~\\cite{wang2025attractor} show that successive paraphrasing converges to low-period attractor cycles; the depth at which our classifier loses discriminability ($\\Kempirical{=}2$ for C4) may correspond to the generation at which outputs enter the attractor basin.\nThe JSD cliff (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}) is consistent with rapid convergence toward a fixed point, though a formal correspondence between attractor entry and $\\Kempirical$ remains to be established.\n\n\\paragraph{Transfer implications for curation.}\nThe 4/6 off-diagonal boundary-level failure at 7B (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}) means that $\\Kempirical{=}2$ cannot be assumed to transfer when the generator identity is unknown.\nMulti-source pooling partially addresses this: domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ is preserved when classifiers are trained on mixed-generator corpora, suggesting that curation pipelines should use multi-source trained classifiers rather than single-model classifiers.\n\n\\paragraph{Mixed-chain scenarios.}\nReal contamination likely involves both processes; our results bound the extremes ($\\Kempirical{=}2$ floor to $\\Kempirical{=}7$ ceiling), but whether prompt-chain laundering masks retrain-chain signals remains open.\n\n",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01VWRHmmxQy1bEHutpkVyK4t`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:why_kstar}
4	\label{sec:one_wash}
5	\label{sec:negative_graduated}
6	\label{sec:repr_dependence}
7	\label{sec:boundary_cases}
8	\label{sec:length_confound}
9	
10	\begin{figure}[t]
11	\centering
12	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}
13	\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 7 continuation-mode C4 models (1B--14B) with $\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\Kempirical{=}7$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\lambda \approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\lambda \approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\lambda = 0.285$).}
14	\label{fig:jsd_decay}
15	\end{figure}
16	
17	\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
18	For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ reflects concentrated information loss: starting from first-generation text ($\auc_{0v1} \geq 0.92$), a single additional generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content (1v2 drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp; 2v3 falls below $\theta$).
19	
20	\paragraph{Operational value of the precise boundary.}
21	Knowing that discrimination ``collapses early'' provides no actionable guidance: it does not distinguish binary from graduated filtering, nor specify at which generation to draw the threshold.
22	$\Kempirical{=}2$ resolves this: for web text, graduated weighting beyond depth~2 offers no advantage over unfiltered baselines (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}), confirming $\Kempirical{=}2$ as the operationally binding boundary.
23	The $\theta$-invariant, feature-set-invariant domain ordering arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 $=$ Code ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$) extends this precision across the domain spectrum (Table~\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}), making it predictive for unseen corpora within these domain types.
24	Contamination rate experiments corroborate this: binary advantage scales with contamination severity while graduated alternatives show no improvement (Appendix~\ref{sec:rate_sweep}).
25	
26	\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\ retrain-chain.}
27	All main results use prompt-chain (a fixed model recursively generating from its own outputs), so $\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects text-intrinsic information loss.
28	Retrain-chain experiments (LoRA fine-tuning each generation) yield $\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, and $\Kempirical{\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), placing prompt-chain $\Kempirical{=}2$ as the curation floor and retrain-chain $\Kempirical{=}7$ as the protocol-dependent upper bound.
29	
30	\paragraph{Wikipedia single-generation saturation.}
31	Wikipedia's template-driven structure causes generated text to converge rapidly toward the domain distribution, consistent with its low TTR and entropy (Table~\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}); the regularity that makes encyclopedic text easy to generate also eliminates depth signal after a single generation.
32	
33	\paragraph{JSD decay and domain dependence.}
34	Token-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff ($\lambda \approx 2.9$), arXiv decays $3\times$ slower ($\lambda \approx 1.0$), and the retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\lambda = 0.285$; Appendix~\ref{sec:jsd_extended}); the ordering correlates with structural complexity (Table~\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).
35	
36	\paragraph{Feature-space ceiling and $\Kempirical_{\text{15D}}$ vs.\ $\Kempirical_{\text{intrinsic}}$.}
37	(a)~The domain ordering (arXiv $>$ C4 $=$ Code $>$ Wikipedia) is \emph{feature-set invariant}: it holds under both 15D statistics and DeBERTa fine-tuning, making it a text-intrinsic structural property.
38	(b)~Absolute $\Kempirical$ values are \emph{feature-dependent}: DeBERTa pushes $\Kempirical$ to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv (Table~\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}), confirming that 15D measurements provide a conservative lower bound on $\Kempirical_{\text{intrinsic}}$.
39	(c)~On C4 and Code, DeBERTa matches the 15D bound ($\Kempirical{=}2$), establishing a \emph{representation-independent boundary}: even 184M-parameter contextual embeddings cannot surpass $\Kempirical{=}2$ on web text; LightGBM 1v2 AUC declines only 0.8\,pp from 1.5B to 32B (three data points, insufficient for extrapolation).
40	DeBERTa-based filtering offers no advantage over 15D OBD on web text (Table~\ref{tab:deberta_filtering}), confirming $\Kempirical{=}2$ as operationally binding regardless of feature expressiveness.
41	
42	\paragraph{Multi-source invariance and implications.}
43	Multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\Kempirical$ across all four domains, confirming $\Kempirical$ as an intrinsic domain property; binary filtering suffices for web text and code ($\Kempirical{=}2$), while academic text ($\Kempirical{=}3$) may benefit from finer stratification.
44	
45	\paragraph{Connection to attractor dynamics.}
46	Wang et al.~\cite{wang2025attractor} show that successive paraphrasing converges to low-period attractor cycles; the depth at which our classifier loses discriminability ($\Kempirical{=}2$ for C4) may correspond to the generation at which outputs enter the attractor basin.
47	The JSD cliff (Figure~\ref{fig:jsd_decay}) is consistent with rapid convergence toward a fixed point, though a formal correspondence between attractor entry and $\Kempirical$ remains to be established.
48	
49	\paragraph{Transfer implications for curation.}
50	The 4/6 off-diagonal boundary-level failure at 7B (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}) means that $\Kempirical{=}2$ cannot be assumed to transfer when the generator identity is unknown.
51	Multi-source pooling partially addresses this: domain-calibrated $\Kempirical$ is preserved when classifiers are trained on mixed-generator corpora, suggesting that curation pipelines should use multi-source trained classifiers rather than single-model classifiers.
52	
53	\paragraph{Mixed-chain scenarios.}
54	Real contamination likely involves both processes; our results bound the extremes ($\Kempirical{=}2$ floor to $\Kempirical{=}7$ ceiling), but whether prompt-chain laundering masks retrain-chain signals remains open.
55	
56	
```

    "newString": "Multi-source pooling partially addresses this: domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ is preserved under mixed-generator training, suggesting multi-source classifiers for curation pipelines.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}\n\\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 7 continuation-mode C4 models (1B--14B) with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\\Kempirical{=}7$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ reflects concentrated information loss: starting from first-generation text ($\\auc_{0v1} \\geq 0.92$), a single additional generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content (1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp; 2v3 falls below $\\theta$).\n\n\\paragraph{Operational value of the precise boundary.}\nKnowing that discrimination ``collapses early'' provides no actionable guidance: it does not distinguish binary from graduated filtering, nor specify at which generation to draw the threshold.\n$\\Kempirical{=}2$ resolves this: for web text, graduated weighting beyond depth~2 offers no advantage over unfiltered baselines (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}), confirming $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as the operationally binding boundary.\nThe $\\theta$-invariant, feature-set-invariant domain ordering arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 $=$ Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) extends this precision across the domain spectrum (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}), making it predictive for unseen corpora within these domain types.\nContamination rate experiments corroborate this: binary advantage scales with contamination severity while graduated alternatives show no improvement (Appendix~\\ref{sec:rate_sweep}).\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\\ retrain-chain.}\nAll main results use prompt-chain (a fixed model recursively generating from its own outputs), so $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects text-intrinsic information loss.\nRetrain-chain experiments (LoRA fine-tuning each generation) yield $\\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, and $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), placing prompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as the curation floor and retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{=}7$ as the protocol-dependent upper bound.\n\n\\paragraph{Wikipedia single-generation saturation.}\nWikipedia's template-driven structure causes generated text to converge rapidly toward the domain distribution, consistent with its low TTR and entropy (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}); the regularity that makes encyclopedic text easy to generate also eliminates depth signal after a single generation.\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay and domain dependence.}\nToken-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.9$), arXiv decays $3\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$), and the retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}); the ordering correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).\n\n\\paragraph{Feature-space ceiling and $\\Kempirical_{\\text{15D}}$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical_{\\text{intrinsic}}$.}\n(a)~The domain ordering (arXiv $>$ C4 $=$ Code $>$ Wikipedia) is \\emph{feature-set invariant}: it holds under both 15D statistics and DeBERTa fine-tuning, making it a text-intrinsic structural property.\n(b)~Absolute $\\Kempirical$ values are \\emph{feature-dependent}: DeBERTa pushes $\\Kempirical$ to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}), confirming that 15D measurements provide a conservative lower bound on $\\Kempirical_{\\text{intrinsic}}$.\n(c)~On C4 and Code, DeBERTa matches the 15D bound ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), establishing a \\emph{representation-independent boundary}: even 184M-parameter contextual embeddings cannot surpass $\\Kempirical{=}2$ on web text; LightGBM 1v2 AUC declines only 0.8\\,pp from 1.5B to 32B (three data points, insufficient for extrapolation).\nDeBERTa-based filtering offers no advantage over 15D OBD on web text (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_filtering}), confirming $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as operationally binding regardless of feature expressiveness.\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-source invariance and implications.}\nMulti-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains, confirming $\\Kempirical$ as an intrinsic domain property; binary filtering suffices for web text and code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), while academic text ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) may benefit from finer stratification.\n\n\\paragraph{Connection to attractor dynamics.}\nWang et al.~\\cite{wang2025attractor} show that successive paraphrasing converges to low-period attractor cycles; the depth at which our classifier loses discriminability ($\\Kempirical{=}2$ for C4) may correspond to the generation at which outputs enter the attractor basin.\nThe JSD cliff (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}) is consistent with rapid convergence toward a fixed point, though a formal correspondence between attractor entry and $\\Kempirical$ remains to be established.\n\n\\paragraph{Transfer implications for curation.}\nThe 4/6 off-diagonal boundary-level failure at 7B (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}) means that $\\Kempirical{=}2$ cannot be assumed to transfer when the generator identity is unknown.\nMulti-source pooling partially addresses this: domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ is preserved when classifiers are trained on mixed-generator corpora, suggesting that curation pipelines should use multi-source trained classifiers rather than single-model classifiers.\n\n\\paragraph{Mixed-chain scenarios.}\nReal contamination likely involves both processes; our results bound the extremes ($\\Kempirical{=}2$ floor to $\\Kempirical{=}7$ ceiling), but whether prompt-chain laundering masks retrain-chain signals remains open.\n\n",

    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

[Director 指令] [Patch] Section 5 主文表格更新 + 叙事调整

**数据已齐全。** 完整 3 seeds × 5 benchmarks × 3 strategies + base model：

| Config | PIQA (acc_norm) | TruthfulQA (mc2) | WinoGrande (acc) | ARC-C (acc_norm) | GSM8K (em) |
|--------|-----------------|-------------------|-------------------|-------------------|------------|
| base_model | 0.7612 | 0.4659 | 0.6456 | 0.5486 | 0.6224 |
| no_filter (3s) | 0.7519±0.22pp | 0.4246±0.28pp | 0.6485±0.32pp | 0.5193±0.58pp | 0.5666±0.53pp |
| binary (3s) | 0.7479±0.16pp | 0.4398±0.51pp | 0.6406±0.20pp | 0.5171±0.38pp | 0.5858±0.05pp |
| graduated (3s) | 0.7510±0.18pp | 0.4245±0.27pp | 0.6461±0.24pp | 0.5190±0.55pp | 0.5613±0.38pp |

**Section 5 叙事调整（核心要求）：**

1. **Primary finding = graduated ≈ no_filter**（K*=2 核心验证）：所有 5 benchmarks Δ ≤ 0.53pp，无显著差异。graduated 不添加任何价值。这直接验证 K* 以上 depth weighting 无信号。

2. **Secondary finding = binary 的 benchmark-dependent 效果**：
   - 正向：GSM8K +1.92pp, TruthfulQA +1.52pp（数学推理 + 事实性）
   - 负向：WinoGrande -0.79pp（commonsense reasoning）
   - 噪声：PIQA -0.40pp, ARC-C -0.22pp
   - Frame 为"binary filtering preserves mathematical reasoning capability"，不 claim uniformly better

3. **所有策略 vs base 退化**：作为一句补充说明（"fine-tuning on curated subsets incurs a general capability cost, which binary filtering partially mitigates for reasoning tasks"）

4. **表格设计**：正文一个表，5 benchmark × 4 rows（base + 3 strategies），mean±std。Table caption 注明每个 benchmark 使用的指标。WinoGrande 的 -0.79pp 必须如实报告。

5. **不要改 Discussion L22**——已从"principled choice"改为"operationally binding boundary"，与新数据一致。

6. **DeBERTa/Pythia appendix 表已完成，不需要再改。**

这次不用验证引用。编译后确认 8 页限制。

1	\section{$\Kempirical$-Consistent Filtering}
2	\label{sec:filtering}
3	
4	The preceding sections establish $\Kempirical{=}2$ as a robust boundary for web text; we now test whether this measurement yields actionable guidance for corpus curation.
5	As a preliminary consistency check (not a standalone evaluation), we test whether $\Kempirical{=}2$ predicts the correct filtering strategy on 30{,}000 prompt-chain documents (depths 0--5; Table~\ref{tab:tab_filtering_main}).
6	Binary filtering reduces MMLU degradation from $-$1.53\,pp to $-$0.77\,pp ($p_{\text{corr}} < 0.05$), outperforming graduated alternatives~\citep{drayson2025, xie2023dsir} that inject noise by weighting depths beyond $\Kempirical$; cross-model replication and a contamination rate sweep (10--100\%) confirm this ordering, with binary advantage scaling with severity while graduated filtering shows no improvement (Appendix~\ref{sec:pythia_filtering}, \ref{sec:dose_response}, \ref{sec:rate_sweep}).
7

1004	The domain ordering (arXiv $>$ C4 $\geq$ Code $>$ Wikipedia) is preserved under the changepoint criterion.

Bold: best strategy.}\n\\label{tab:tab_filtering_main}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\input{tables/tab_filtering_main}}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe preceding sections establish $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as a robust boundary; we test whether this predicts the correct curation strategy by fine-tuning Qwen-2.5-1.5B on 30{,}000 prompt-chain documents (depths 0--5; Table~\\ref{tab:tab_filtering_main}).\nGraduated filtering performs indistinguishably from no filtering ($\\Delta \\leq 0.53$\\,pp across five benchmarks), confirming that depth weighting beyond $\\Kempirical$ captures no signal; binary filtering selectively preserves mathematical reasoning (GSM8K $+$1.92\\,pp) and factual accuracy (TruthfulQA $+$1.52\\,pp) but degrades commonsense reasoning (WinoGrande $-$0.79\\,pp).\nAll strategies incur capability cost relative to the base model; binary filtering partially mitigates this for reasoning tasks.\nCross-model replication and rate sweeps (10--100\\%) confirm graduated $\\approx$ no-filter ordering (Appendix~\\ref{sec:pythia_filtering}, \\ref{sec:dose_response}, \\ref{sec:rate_sweep}).\n"
}

  "content": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}\n\\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 7 continuation-mode C4 models (1B--14B) with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\\Kempirical{=}7$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ reflects concentrated information loss: starting from first-generation text ($\\auc_{0v1} \\geq 0.92$), a single additional generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content (1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp; 2v3 falls below $\\theta$).\n\n\\paragraph{Operational value of the precise boundary.}\nKnowing that discrimination ``collapses early'' provides no actionable guidance.\n$\\Kempirical{=}2$ resolves this: graduated weighting beyond depth~2 offers no advantage (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}), and the $\\theta$-invariant domain ordering arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 $=$ Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) extends this across domains (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}).\nRate sweeps corroborate: binary advantage scales with contamination severity while graduated alternatives show no improvement (Appendix~\\ref{sec:rate_sweep}).\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\\ retrain-chain.}\nAll main results use prompt-chain, so $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects text-intrinsic information loss.\nRetrain-chain experiments yield $\\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, and $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), placing prompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as the curation floor and retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{=}7$ as the upper bound.\n\n\\paragraph{Wikipedia single-generation saturation.}\nWikipedia's template-driven structure causes rapid convergence toward the domain distribution (low TTR and entropy; Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}), eliminating depth signal after a single generation.\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay and domain dependence.}\nToken-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.9$), arXiv decays $3\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$), and the retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}); the ordering correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).\n\n\\paragraph{Feature-space ceiling and $\\Kempirical_{\\text{15D}}$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical_{\\text{intrinsic}}$.}\n(a)~The domain ordering is \\emph{feature-set invariant}: it holds under both 15D statistics and DeBERTa fine-tuning.\n(b)~Absolute $\\Kempirical$ values are \\emph{feature-dependent}: DeBERTa extends $\\Kempirical$ by $+1$ on Wikipedia and arXiv (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}), so 15D provides a conservative lower bound.\n(c)~On C4 and Code, DeBERTa matches the 15D bound ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), establishing a \\emph{representation-independent boundary}; DeBERTa-based filtering offers no advantage over 15D OBD (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_filtering}), confirming $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as operationally binding regardless of feature expressiveness.\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-source invariance.}\nMulti-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains; binary filtering suffices for web text and code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), while academic text ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) may benefit from finer stratification.\n\n\\paragraph{Transfer implications for curation.}\nThe 4/6 off-diagonal boundary-level failure at 7B (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}) means that $\\Kempirical{=}2$ cannot be assumed to transfer when the generator identity is unknown.\nMulti-source pooling partially addresses this: domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ is preserved under mixed-generator training, suggesting multi-source classifiers for curation pipelines.\n\n\\paragraph{Mixed-chain scenarios.}\nReal contamination likely involves both processes; our results bound the extremes ($\\Kempirical{=}2$ to $\\Kempirical{=}7$), but whether prompt-chain laundering masks retrain-chain signals remains open.\n\n"
}

          "+All main results use prompt-chain, so $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects text-intrinsic information loss.",

          " \\paragraph{Wikipedia single-generation saturation.}",
          "-Wikipedia's template-driven structure causes generated text to converge rapidly toward the domain distribution, consistent with its low TTR and entropy (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}); the regularity that makes encyclopedic text easy to generate also eliminates depth signal after a single generation.",
          "+Wikipedia's template-driven structure causes rapid convergence toward the domain distribution (low TTR and entropy; Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}), eliminating depth signal after a single generation.",

          " \\paragraph{JSD decay and domain dependence.}",
          " Token-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.9$), arXiv decays $3\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$), and the retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}); the ordering correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Feature-space ceiling and $\\Kempirical_{\\text{15D}}$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical_{\\text{intrinsic}}$.}",
          "-(a)~The domain ordering (arXiv $>$ C4 $=$ Code $>$ Wikipedia) is \\emph{feature-set invariant}: it holds under both 15D statistics and DeBERTa fine-tuning, making it a text-intrinsic structural property.",
          "-(b)~Absolute $\\Kempirical$ values are \\emph{feature-dependent}: DeBERTa pushes $\\Kempirical$ to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}), confirming that 15D measurements provide a conservative lower bound on $\\Kempirical_{\\text{intrinsic}}$.",
          "-(c)~On C4 and Code, DeBERTa matches the 15D bound ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), establishing a \\emph{representation-independent boundary}: even 184M-parameter contextual embeddings cannot surpass $\\Kempirical{=}2$ on web text; LightGBM 1v2 AUC declines only 0.8\\,pp from 1.5B to 32B (three data points, insufficient for extrapolation).",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}\n\\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 7 continuation-mode C4 models (1B--14B) with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\\Kempirical{=}7$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ reflects concentrated information loss: starting from first-generation text ($\\auc_{0v1} \\geq 0.92$), a single additional generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content (1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp; 2v3 falls below $\\theta$).\n\n\\paragraph{Operational value of the precise boundary.}\nKnowing that discrimination ``collapses early'' provides no actionable guidance: it does not distinguish binary from graduated filtering, nor specify at which generation to draw the threshold.\n$\\Kempirical{=}2$ resolves this: for web text, graduated weighting beyond depth~2 offers no advantage over unfiltered baselines (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}), confirming $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as the operationally binding boundary.\nThe $\\theta$-invariant, feature-set-invariant domain ordering arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 $=$ Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) extends this precision across the domain spectrum (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}), making it predictive for unseen corpora within these domain types.\nContamination rate experiments corroborate this: binary advantage scales with contamination severity while graduated alternatives show no improvement (Appendix~\\ref{sec:rate_sweep}).\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain vs.\\ retrain-chain.}\nAll main results use prompt-chain (a fixed model recursively generating from its own outputs), so $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects text-intrinsic information loss.\nRetrain-chain experiments (LoRA fine-tuning each generation) yield $\\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, and $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), placing prompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as the curation floor and retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{=}7$ as the protocol-dependent upper bound.\n\n\\paragraph{Wikipedia single-generation saturation.}\nWikipedia's template-driven structure causes generated text to converge rapidly toward the domain distribution, consistent with its low TTR and entropy (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}); the regularity that makes encyclopedic text easy to generate also eliminates depth signal after a single generation.\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay and domain dependence.}\nToken-level JSD reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 exhibits a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.9$), arXiv decays $3\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$), and the retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$; Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}); the ordering correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).\n\n\\paragraph{Feature-space ceiling and $\\Kempirical_{\\text{15D}}$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical_{\\text{intrinsic}}$.}\n(a)~The domain ordering (arXiv $>$ C4 $=$ Code $>$ Wikipedia) is \\emph{feature-set invariant}: it holds under both 15D statistics and DeBERTa fine-tuning, making it a text-intrinsic structural property.\n(b)~Absolute $\\Kempirical$ values are \\emph{feature-dependent}: DeBERTa pushes $\\Kempirical$ to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_arxiv}), confirming that 15D measurements provide a conservative lower bound on $\\Kempirical_{\\text{intrinsic}}$.\n(c)~On C4 and Code, DeBERTa matches the 15D bound ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), establishing a \\emph{representation-independent boundary}: even 184M-parameter contextual embeddings cannot surpass $\\Kempirical{=}2$ on web text; LightGBM 1v2 AUC declines only 0.8\\,pp from 1.5B to 32B (three data points, insufficient for extrapolation).\nDeBERTa-based filtering offers no advantage over 15D OBD on web text (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_filtering}), confirming $\\Kempirical{=}2$ as operationally binding regardless of feature expressiveness.\n\n\\paragraph{Multi-source invariance and implications.}\nMulti-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains, confirming $\\Kempirical$ as an intrinsic domain property; binary filtering suffices for web text and code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), while academic text ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) may benefit from finer stratification.\n\n\\paragraph{Connection to attractor dynamics.}\nWang et al.~\\cite{wang2025attractor} show that successive paraphrasing converges to low-period attractor cycles; the depth at which our classifier loses discriminability ($\\Kempirical{=}2$ for C4) may correspond to the generation at which outputs enter the attractor basin.\nThe JSD cliff (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}) is consistent with rapid convergence toward a fixed point, though a formal correspondence between attractor entry and $\\Kempirical$ remains to be established.\n\n\\paragraph{Transfer implications for curation.}\nThe 4/6 off-diagonal boundary-level failure at 7B (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}) means that $\\Kempirical{=}2$ cannot be assumed to transfer when the generator identity is unknown.\nMulti-source pooling partially addresses this: domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ is preserved under mixed-generator training, suggesting multi-source classifiers for curation pipelines.\n\n\\paragraph{Mixed-chain scenarios.}\nReal contamination likely involves both processes; our results bound the extremes ($\\Kempirical{=}2$ floor to $\\Kempirical{=}7$ ceiling), but whether prompt-chain laundering masks retrain-chain signals remains open.\n\n",
    "userModified": false
  },

|----------|------|-----|-------|-------|-------|

29	\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*),nosep]
30	  \item \textbf{First empirical measurement of $\Kempirical$} across 10 model configurations (6 architecture families, 1B--14B), 4 source domains (3 text + 1 code), and 4 decoding strategies at $\theta{=}0.60$: four of six families (7/9 continuation-mode configurations) yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ on C4 ($p \leq 10^{-4}$; changepoint-confirmed; margin stratification in Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}); multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\Kempirical$ across all four domains ($+0.010$ mean $\auc$ for generator identity across four domains).
31	  Zero-shot frontier LLMs likewise collapse beyond $\Kempirical$: per-depth recall at depths $\geq 2$ ranges from 8--38\% (vs.\ 20\% chance), indicating error redistribution rather than genuine depth recovery (Section~\ref{sec:why_kstar}).
32	  \item \textbf{Feature-set-invariant domain ordering}: arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across tested configurations under both 15D statistics and DeBERTa fine-tuning; absolute $\Kempirical$ values are feature-dependent (15D provides a conservative lower bound), but the ordering is robust (Section~\ref{sec:cross_domain}).
33	  \item \textbf{Transfer and consistency check}: largely generator-independent features within continuation mode enable 80--92\% accuracy retention among tested configurations ($\rho > 0.90$; Section~\ref{sec:transfer}), though boundary-level $\Kempirical$ assignments are fragile under cross-model transfer (4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\theta$); as a consistency check, domain-appropriate $\Kempirical$ correctly predicts binary ${>}$ graduated filtering for web text (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}).
34	\end{enumerate}
```

---

**Batch 1: Must-Fix**

**1A. Introduction 重写（C2: prompt-chain vs collapse 张力）**
第一段需要更明确区分：
- (a) prompt-chain（文本统计特征衰减，fixed model）vs retrain-chain（权重累积偏移）
- (b) prompt-chain 对 web-scale curation 的实践相关性（互联网上大量文本是 LLM 一次性生成的，不涉及 retrain）
- (c) 末尾加 retrain-chain K*=7 前瞻引用，形成 K*∈[2,7] 完整图景
- 不要在 Abstract 提 retrain-chain（保持聚焦）

**1B. θ=0.60 LOFO 展示强化（C1 部分修复）**
Method section 中现有 LOFO 句（"A leave-one-family-out cross-validation yields θ̂∈[0.5725,0.6004] with 8/9 K* predictions matching"）扩展为 2-3 句，更显著地作为 circularity defense：
- 强调这是 held-out validation（θ 从 4 个 families 推导，在第 5 个上验证）
- 报告 θ̂ range 包含 0.60
- 引用 Appendix 的完整 LOFO 表

**1C. 弱化可推广性声称（C3-A）**
- Abstract/Introduction/Experiments 中所有 "6 architecture families" 类表述加限定："6 families (with Qwen variants comprising half the tested configurations)"
- 加 Limitations 条目："Model-family balance: Qwen variants account for 5 of 10 configurations, so K*=2 generalizability to underrepresented families warrants further validation."
- ≥7B 结论限定："three ≥7B models tested"

---

**Batch 2: Should-Fix**

**2A. K* 符号系统化（W1 + W5）**
- 主文统一为 K*（默认 15D+OBD），仅在 DeBERTa 对比时引入 K*_{15D} 和 K*_{DeBERTa}
- 搜索并删除/合并冗余变体 K*_theory, K*_neural, K*_binary（如有）
- Discussion 新增 "Practical Recommendations" 段落（2-3 句）：C4/Code 用 K*=2（15D 或 DeBERTa 均可）；arXiv 用 K*_DeBERTa=4 更准确但需 fine-tuned encoder；实践者应视资源选择

**2B. Section 5 标题 + 补充（W2）**
- 标题改为 "Consistency Check: $\Kempirical$-Guided Filtering"（从 "K*-Consistent Filtering"）
- Section 5 开头加 1 句 power caveat："(30K documents × 3 seeds; effect sizes ≤2pp may be underpowered)"
- Limitations 补充 power analysis 声明

**2C. Abstract 精简（W3）**
- 加 K* 直觉定义在首次使用处（"K*, the maximum generational depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations"）
- 移除 Section 内部引用
- 精简实现细节（OBD-LightGBM → "ordinal classifier"；去 margin stratification 术语）

**2D. Conclusion 扩展（W4）**
扩展至 8-12 行，加入：
- K*=2 对 web-scale curation 的 actionable takeaway
- graduated≈no_filter 的 take-home message
- prompt-chain K*=2 vs retrain-chain K*=7 的 broader implications
- 2 句 future directions（scaling beyond 14B, mixed chains, domain-adaptive thresholds）
- 空间来源：检查 Discussion 是否有可以移到 Appendix 的细节（JSD decay 具体数值？Attractor dynamics 段落之前被 Paper Agent 删了所以已经压过）。如果 Discussion 无法再压，检查 Related Work 或 Method 中的冗余。

---

**Batch 3: Minor**

**3A. Fu et al. (2025) 引用**
- 引用信息：Fu et al. "A Theoretical Perspective: How to Prevent Model Collapse in Self-consuming Training Loops" ICLR 2025, arXiv:2502.18865
- 加到 references.bib
- 在 Related Work "Model collapse and recursive degradation" 段中引用

**3B. Orphan bib 清理**
- 删除 hao2025rewrite, tang2024science, wu2023survey
- 确认 levin2017markov 年份和版本正确

**3C. ANOVA 措辞（M3）**
- 全文 "generator-independent" → "largely generator-invariant"

**3D. Retrain-chain CI（M4）**
- 检查 Appendix 中是否已有 t-distribution CI 的理由说明（之前 Round 22 已修改过）

**3E. Q3 Domain-model interaction**

**3F. Q5/Q6 Limitations 补充**
- Q5: Mixed chain K* estimation 声明
- Q6: "Larger encoders (DeBERTa-large/xl) may further tighten the K* upper bound"

**3G. 孤儿表格文件清理**

**注意事项：**
- 8 页主文硬限制——扩展 Conclusion/LOFO/Practical Recommendations 需要在别处压缩
- 不要在 Abstract 提 retrain-chain
- 引用需要验证（Fu et al. 2025）

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_016LyNn9H94L365Swj7XUEZx`</summary>

```text
37	\begin{abstract}
38	Web-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.
39	We measure the discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$ under prompt-chain generation using generator-independent statistical features and an OBD-LightGBM classifier.
40	The $\theta$-invariant domain ordering arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $=$ Code ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$) holds across models ($\leq$14B); within this hierarchy, $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ for web text emerges as the dominant mode (four of six architecture families; changepoint-confirmed; margin stratification in Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).
41	The domain ordering is feature-set invariant (preserved under both 15D statistics and DeBERTa fine-tuning), while absolute $\Kempirical$ values are feature-dependent: DeBERTa matches the 15D bound on C4 and Code but extends $\Kempirical$ to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv, placing 15D measurements as conservative lower bounds.
42	Multi-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\Kempirical$ across all four domains with negligible generator-identity signal; features transfer across generators (80--92\% aggregate accuracy, driven by high 0v1 separability), but boundary-level $\Kempirical$ assignments fail under cross-model transfer (4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\theta$).
43	As a consistency check, $\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts that binary filtering outperforms graduated alternatives for web text.
44	\end{abstract}
45

within this hierarchy, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ for web text emerges as the dominant mode (four of six architecture families; changepoint-confirmed; margin stratification in Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\nThe domain ordering is feature-set invariant (preserved under both 15D statistics and DeBERTa fine-tuning), while absolute $\\Kempirical$ values are feature-dependent: DeBERTa matches the 15D bound on C4 and Code but extends $\\Kempirical$ to 3 on Wikipedia and 4 on arXiv, placing 15D measurements as conservative lower bounds.\nMulti-source pooling preserves domain-calibrated $\\Kempirical$ across all four domains with negligible generator-identity signal; features transfer across generators (80--92\\% aggregate accuracy, driven by high 0v1 separability), but boundary-level $\\Kempirical$ assignments fail under cross-model transfer (4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\\theta$).\nAs a consistency check, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts that binary filtering outperforms graduated alternatives for web text.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n% === Section 1: Introduction ===\n\\input{introduction}\n\n% === Section 2: Related Work ===\n\\input{related_work}\n\n% === Section 3: Method ===\n\\input{method}\n\n% === Section 4: Experiments ===\n\\input{experiments_c1}\n\n% === Section 5: K*-Aware Data Filtering ===\n\\input{experiments_c3}\n\n% === Section 6: Discussion ===\n\\input{discussion}\n\n% === Section 7: Conclusion ===\n\\input{conclusion}\n",

65	\input{conclusion}
66	
67	% === Limitations (ACL required, unnumbered) ===
68	\section*{Limitations}
69	
70	Our findings are bounded by:
71	(1)~\emph{Scope}: full 10-configuration characterization at $\leq$14B (6 architecture families; Gemma-2-2B and Mistral-7B competence-limited at $\Kempirical{=}1$), with 32B-scale validation via LightGBM and DeBERTa (Section~\ref{sec:neural_probe}); English text (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv) and Python code (CodeSearchNet), four decoding strategies; arXiv $\Kempirical{=}3$ is confirmed with five models across four architecture families but with reduced sample size ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model); code domain tested with five models across four families (Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B-v0.3, $\Kempirical{=}2$; Pythia-1.4B, $\Kempirical{=}1$ with 1v2 CI upper bound $> \theta$); our Code domain uses CodeSearchNet (2019), which may not fully represent current LLM-generated code patterns; subsampling analysis indicates $n{\geq}2{,}000$ suffices for 100\% reliable $\Kempirical$ determination (Appendix~\ref{sec:power_curve}).
72	The 1.5B$\to$32B trend shows stable $\Kempirical{=}2$ with declining 1v2 AUC; whether this holds at 70B+ scales remains unverified.
73	(2)~\emph{Protocol}: all main experiments use prompt-chain, which isolates text-intrinsic degradation from training dynamics; real-world contamination involves both prompting and retraining, and retrain-chain yields $\Kempirical{=}7$ for Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B, $\Kempirical{\geq}4$ for OLMo-1B (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), so prompt-chain $\Kempirical$ may underestimate discriminability in mixed settings.
74	Mixed prompt-chain and retrain-chain scenarios, which better approximate real contamination pathways, are not addressed; preliminary bounds are discussed in Section~\ref{sec:discussion}.
75	We focus on continuation mode as a controlled setting; instruction-tuned models introduce system prompts and RLHF-induced stylistic shifts that confound depth estimation.
76	(3)~\emph{Threshold}: $\Kempirical$ ranges from 2--3 at $\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\theta{=}0.70$; 7/9 continuation-mode configurations yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ (Gemma-2-2B, Mistral-7B: $\Kempirical{=}1$); a $\theta$-free changepoint analysis agrees within $\pm 1$ for 35/37 configurations (Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).
77	(4)~\emph{Marginal boundary}: Qwen-continuation's 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\theta$ by only 0.002; cross-scorer validation drops this to $\Kempirical{=}1$ (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}). LLaMA-3.1-8B (C4) and Mistral-7B-v0.3 (code) represent the most marginal $\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments, with 1v2 CI lower bounds within 0.3 percentage points of $\theta$.
78	(5)~\emph{Feature set}: the 15D features yield a feature-set-specific lower bound ($\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher); $K^*_{\text{neural}}$ is model-dependent ($3$ for Qwen, $2$ for Pythia/OLMo).
79	(6)~\emph{Segment length}: $\Kempirical{=}2$ confirmed for 128 and 256 tokens (CI lower $> \theta$); 512 tokens yields point estimate $\Kempirical{=}2$ but CI lower marginally below $\theta$ ($0.5979$); the boundary weakens at longer generation lengths.
80	(7)~\emph{Curation applicability}: the filtering evaluation is validated on two architectures (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct and Pythia-1.4B) with consistent strategy ordering (Appendix~\ref{sec:pythia_filtering}), though absolute improvement remains small and effect sizes diminish at lower contamination ratios (10--50\%; Appendix~\ref{sec:dose_response}).
81	(8)~\emph{Family balance}: the Qwen2.5 family provides 5 of 10 total configurations (4 of 9 continuation-mode); though spanning three scales (1.5B, 7B, 14B) and two training variants (base, instruct), aggregate statistics weight this family more heavily than the five single-configuration families.
82	The pipeline scales linearly with corpus size; the computational bottleneck is scorer inference, which can be parallelized across documents.
83	(9)~\emph{Transfer coverage}: cross-model transfer validation shows 80--92\% accuracy retention at $\leq$1.5B (4 configurations) and 96--100\% mean pairwise AUC retention at 7B (3 models, 3 architecture families; Appendix~\ref{sec:7b_transfer}); however, at the $\Kempirical$ boundary (1v2), 4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\theta$, indicating that boundary-level transfer remains fragile even as aggregate transfer strengthens with scale.
84	Additional limitations (cross-model scoring sensitivity, feature ranking variability at the 1v2 boundary, structural composition confound analysis) appear in Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.
85	
86	% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===
87	\section*{Ethics Statement}
88	
89	Generational depth estimation is intended for data curation and corpus auditing, positive-sum applications that improve training data quality.
90	Our experiments use publicly available corpora (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv abstracts) and do not involve personally identifiable information.
91	A potential dual-use concern is that depth estimation techniques could inform adversarial strategies to evade detection (e.g., by calibrating rewriting to mimic earlier-generation statistics); we note that this risk is shared by all detection research and that the binary detection boundary ($\auc \geq 0.96$ at 0v1) remains robust.
92	Code and generated corpora will be released upon acceptance to enable reproducibility.
93	
94	\bibliography{references}
95	
96	\input{appendix}
97	\input{appendix_extended}
98	
99	\end{document}
100

```text
1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Problem Formulation}
5	\label{sec:formulation}
6	
7	A generation operator $G_\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\delta$) produces successive generations $X \to Y_1 \to \cdots \to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \triangleq X$ (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
8	This sequence forms a Markov chain.
9	By the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \geq I(X; Y_2) \geq \cdots \geq 0$: the original signal degrades monotonically.
10	Together with Markov chain convergence, this implies a finite $\Ktheory$ beyond which adjacent generations become indistinguishable; DPI motivates finite $\Ktheory$ but does not formally guarantee or bound it.
11	Specifically, DPI constrains the cumulative divergence from the seed text; the adjacent-pair convergence that determines $\Kempirical$ follows from the chain's approach to its stationary distribution~\cite{levin2017markov}, where successive transition distributions become indistinguishable.
12	The actual saturation depth must therefore be measured empirically.
13	
14	We define the operational lower bound $K^*_{\text{empirical}}$ (hereafter $\Kempirical$), where $S$ denotes the feature set (specified in Section~\ref{sec:features}):
15	\begin{equation}
16	  \Kempirical = \max\!\bigl\{k \!\geq\! 1 : \mathrm{CI}_{\mathrm{low}}(\auc_{j\text{-}1,j}) > \theta,\ \forall j \!\leq\! k\bigr\},
17	  \label{eq:kstar}
18	\end{equation}
19	where $\mathrm{CI}_{\mathrm{low}}(\auc_{k-1,k})$ is the lower bound of a 95\% bootstrap confidence interval (1{,}000 resamples\footnote{We use 1{,}000 resamples as the default for computational efficiency; arXiv cross-scorer CIs (Table~\ref{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}) use 10{,}000 to achieve tighter bounds at marginal boundaries where sub-pp differences determine the $\Kempirical{=}2$ vs.\ $\Kempirical{=}3$ verdict.}) over the pairwise AUC (Area Under the ROC Curve) between depths $k{-}1$ and $k$, and $\theta = 0.60$ is a post-hoc operational threshold (motivated below).
20	This criterion ensures that $\Kempirical$ reflects statistically robust discriminability; the $\forall j \leq k$ constraint is naturally satisfied because $\auc_{k,k+1}$ decreases monotonically above $\theta$ across all tested configurations (Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci}).
21	While non-monotonic $\auc_{k,k+1}$ is theoretically possible if intermediate generations traverse a high-entropy transient region, we observe strict monotonicity above $\theta$ across all 37 configurations (Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci_extended}).
22	$\Kempirical$ is jointly conditioned on the feature set and $\theta$; the domain ordering is stable across $\theta \in [0.575, 0.65]$ (Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}). We verify that non-boundary CI upper bounds fall below $\theta$.
23	
24	\paragraph{Threshold choice.}
25	We set $\theta{=}0.60$ at the empirical AUC discontinuity (Figure~\ref{fig:kstar_theta}): 1v2 values cluster at $0.61$--$0.70$ while 2v3 values cluster at $0.55$--$0.59$, creating a ${\sim}$5--11\,pp gap; a 10{,}000-iteration permutation test confirms $p \leq 10^{-4}$ for all boundary comparisons (Benjamini-Hochberg $q_{\max} = 0.003$ across $m{=}30$ tests; Appendix~\ref{sec:bh_correction}).
26	A Youden's J analysis independently validates this choice: the optimal threshold separating boundary from non-boundary AUC values falls at $\theta^*{=}0.605$ ($J{=}1.0$ at config level; $J{=}0.88$ at fold level), placing $\theta{=}0.60$ within the optimal interval $(0.595, 0.611)$~\cite{youden1950index}.
27	We select $\theta$ at this discontinuity because our goal is to identify a natural gap in discriminability rather than to maximize classification performance.
28	$\Kempirical{=}2$ is stable across $\theta \in [0.575, 0.625]$ for all but one marginal configuration (Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci_extended}); only at extremes does $\Kempirical$ shift ($\theta{=}0.55$: $\Kempirical{=}3$ for Qwen-rewrite; $\theta{=}0.70$: $\Kempirical{=}1$).
29	The downstream filtering evaluation (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}) uses OBD without re-optimizing $\theta$, avoiding circularity.
30	A leave-one-family-out (LOFO) cross-validation yields $\hat{\theta} \in [0.5725, 0.6004]$ with 8/9 $\Kempirical$ predictions matching; arXiv corroborates with 6/6 agreement (Appendix~\ref{sec:theta_lofo}).
31	
32	
33	\subsection{Features and Classifier}
34	\label{sec:features}
35	
36	Each document is characterized by a 15-dimensional feature vector spanning four groups: \emph{surprisal moments} (mean, std, skewness, kurtosis; 4~dims), \emph{surprisal derivatives} (mean and std of first- and second-order differences, plus first-order skewness; 5~dims), \emph{vocabulary diversity} (type-token ratio, hapax legomena ratio, self-BLEU; 3~dims), and \emph{frequency tail statistics} (frequency kurtosis, entropy, rare token fraction; 3~dims).
37	All features are computed from token-level surprisal values via an external scorer model (Table~\ref{tab:s5_features}).
38	Self-BLEU is computed within each CV fold to prevent leakage; removing it reduces mean $\auc_{1v2}$ by ${<}\,1$\,pp ($\Kempirical$ unchanged; Table~\ref{tab:selfbleu_ablation}).
39	Hand-crafted features enable cross-generator transfer and permit attribution of discriminative power to specific text properties.
40	These features serve as empirically validated proxies for distributional shift across generations, rather than direct measures of mutual information; the monotonic AUC decay observed across all configurations (Table~\ref{tab:kstar_ci}) provides post-hoc validation.
41	
42	We use \emph{Ordinal Binary Decomposition} (OBD), decomposing the $(K{+}1)$-class problem into $K$ cumulative binary sub-classifiers, each estimating $P(\text{depth} > k \mid \mathbf{x})$ via LightGBM~\cite{ke2017lightgbm} (hyperparameters in Table~\ref{tab:s6_lgbm_params}); OBD achieves lower MAE than multiclass LightGBM (0.984 vs.\ 1.016) and superior pairwise $\auc$ (0.6095 vs.\ 0.4883 for ordinal logistic regression).
43	
44	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
45	\label{sec:experimental_setup}
46	
47	We evaluate ten configurations spanning six architecture families (1B--14B; Table~\ref{tab:setup}), each generating $K{=}5$ depth levels from 5{,}000 C4~\cite{raffel2020t5} seeds (256 tokens; 30{,}000 documents per configuration).
48	Default decoding: nucleus $p{=}0.95$, $T{=}1.0$; ablation covers greedy, $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$.
49	Continuation mode truncates seeds to 128 tokens and extends to 256; rewrite mode paraphrases the full text.
50	Evaluation: 5-fold GroupKFold cross-validation grouped by seed document; pairwise $\auc$ as primary metric.
51	
52	\begin{table}[t]
53	\centering
54	\small
55	\caption{Experimental configurations for depth-chain generation. \textbf{Config}: shorthand identifier; \textbf{Model}: architecture and parameter count; \textbf{Mode}: generation strategy (continuation or rewrite); $N$: number of seed documents per depth level.}
56	\label{tab:setup}
57	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
58	\begin{tabular}{llcc}
59	\toprule
60	\textbf{Config} & \textbf{Model} & \textbf{Mode} & $N$ \\
61	\midrule
62	Qwen-rw   & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Rewrite & 5{,}000 \\
63	Qwen-cont & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
64	Qwen-base & Qwen2.5-1.5B          & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
65	Pythia    & Pythia-1.4B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
66	OLMo      & OLMo-1B               & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
67	Gemma     & Gemma-2-2B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
68	Qwen-7B   & Qwen2.5-7B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
69	Qwen-14B  & Qwen2.5-14B           & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
70	LLaMA-8B  & LLaMA-3.1-8B          & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
71	Mistral   & Mistral-7B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\
72	\bottomrule
73	\end{tabular}}
74	\end{table}
75

      "content": "\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Problem Formulation}\n\\label{sec:formulation}\n\nA generation operator $G_\\delta$ (LLM + decoding strategy $\\delta$) produces successive generations $X \\to Y_1 \\to \\cdots \\to Y_K$, where $Y_k = G_\\delta(Y_{k-1})$ and $Y_0 \\triangleq X$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\nThis sequence forms a Markov chain.\nBy the Data Processing Inequality, $I(X; Y_1) \\geq I(X; Y_2) \\geq \\cdots \\geq 0$: the original signal degrades monotonically.\nTogether with Markov chain convergence, this implies a finite $\\Ktheory$ beyond which adjacent generations become indistinguishable; DPI motivates finite $\\Ktheory$ but does not formally guarantee or bound it.\nSpecifically, DPI constrains the cumulative divergence from the seed text; the adjacent-pair convergence that determines $\\Kempirical$ follows from the chain's approach to its stationary distribution~\\cite{levin2017markov}, where successive transition distributions become indistinguishable.\nThe actual saturation depth must therefore be measured empirically.\n\nWe define the operational lower bound $K^*_{\\text{empirical}}$ (hereafter $\\Kempirical$), where $S$ denotes the feature set (specified in Section~\\ref{sec:features}):\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\Kempirical = \\max\\!\\bigl\\{k \\!\\geq\\! 1 : \\mathrm{CI}_{\\mathrm{low}}(\\auc_{j\\text{-}1,j}) > \\theta,\\ \\forall j \\!\\leq\\! k\\bigr\\},\n  \\label{eq:kstar}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathrm{CI}_{\\mathrm{low}}(\\auc_{k-1,k})$ is the lower bound of a 95\\% bootstrap confidence interval (1{,}000 resamples\\footnote{We use 1{,}000 resamples as the default for computational efficiency; arXiv cross-scorer CIs (Table~\\ref{tab:arxiv_crossscorer_ci}) use 10{,}000 to achieve tighter bounds at marginal boundaries where sub-pp differences determine the $\\Kempirical{=}2$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical{=}3$ verdict.}) over the pairwise AUC (Area Under the ROC Curve) between depths $k{-}1$ and $k$, and $\\theta = 0.60$ is a post-hoc operational threshold (motivated below).\nThis criterion ensures that $\\Kempirical$ reflects statistically robust discriminability; the $\\forall j \\leq k$ constraint is naturally satisfied because $\\auc_{k,k+1}$ decreases monotonically above $\\theta$ across all tested configurations (Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci}).\nWhile non-monotonic $\\auc_{k,k+1}$ is theoretically possible if intermediate generations traverse a high-entropy transient region, we observe strict monotonicity above $\\theta$ across all 37 configurations (Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci_extended}).\n$\\Kempirical$ is jointly conditioned on the feature set and $\\theta$; the domain ordering is stable across $\\theta \\in [0.575, 0.65]$ (Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}). We verify that non-boundary CI upper bounds fall below $\\theta$.\n\n\\paragraph{Threshold choice.}\nWe set $\\theta{=}0.60$ at the empirical AUC discontinuity (Figure~\\ref{fig:kstar_theta}): 1v2 values cluster at $0.61$--$0.70$ while 2v3 values cluster at $0.55$--$0.59$, creating a ${\\sim}$5--11\\,pp gap; a 10{,}000-iteration permutation test confirms $p \\leq 10^{-4}$ for all boundary comparisons (Benjamini-Hochberg $q_{\\max} = 0.003$ across $m{=}30$ tests; Appendix~\\ref{sec:bh_correction}).\nA Youden's J analysis independently validates this choice: the optimal threshold separating boundary from non-boundary AUC values falls at $\\theta^*{=}0.605$ ($J{=}1.0$ at config level; $J{=}0.88$ at fold level), placing $\\theta{=}0.60$ within the optimal interval $(0.595, 0.611)$~\\cite{youden1950index}.\nWe select $\\theta$ at this discontinuity because our goal is to identify a natural gap in discriminability rather than to maximize classification performance.\n$\\Kempirical{=}2$ is stable across $\\theta \\in [0.575, 0.625]$ for all but one marginal configuration (Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci_extended}); only at extremes does $\\Kempirical$ shift ($\\theta{=}0.55$: $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for Qwen-rewrite; $\\theta{=}0.70$: $\\Kempirical{=}1$).\nThe downstream filtering evaluation (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}) uses OBD without re-optimizing $\\theta$, avoiding circularity.\nA leave-one-family-out (LOFO) cross-validation yields $\\hat{\\theta} \\in [0.5725, 0.6004]$ with 8/9 $\\Kempirical$ predictions matching; arXiv corroborates with 6/6 agreement (Appendix~\\ref{sec:theta_lofo}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Features and Classifier}\n\\label{sec:features}\n\nEach document is characterized by a 15-dimensional feature vector spanning four groups: \\emph{surprisal moments} (mean, std, skewness, kurtosis; 4~dims), \\emph{surprisal derivatives} (mean and std of first- and second-order differences, plus first-order skewness; 5~dims), \\emph{vocabulary diversity} (type-token ratio, hapax legomena ratio, self-BLEU; 3~dims), and \\emph{frequency tail statistics} (frequency kurtosis, entropy, rare token fraction; 3~dims).\nAll features are computed from token-level surprisal values via an external scorer model (Table~\\ref{tab:s5_features}).\nSelf-BLEU is computed within each CV fold to prevent leakage; removing it reduces mean $\\auc_{1v2}$ by ${<}\\,1$\\,pp ($\\Kempirical$ unchanged; Table~\\ref{tab:selfbleu_ablation}).\nHand-crafted features enable cross-generator transfer and permit attribution of discriminative power to specific text properties.\nThese features serve as empirically validated proxies for distributional shift across generations, rather than direct measures of mutual information; the monotonic AUC decay observed across all configurations (Table~\\ref{tab:kstar_ci}) provides post-hoc validation.\n\nWe use \\emph{Ordinal Binary Decomposition} (OBD), decomposing the $(K{+}1)$-class problem into $K$ cumulative binary sub-classifiers, each estimating $P(\\text{depth} > k \\mid \\mathbf{x})$ via LightGBM~\\cite{ke2017lightgbm} (hyperparameters in Table~\\ref{tab:s6_lgbm_params}); OBD achieves lower MAE than multiclass LightGBM (0.984 vs.\\ 1.016) and superior pairwise $\\auc$ (0.6095 vs.\\ 0.4883 for ordinal logistic regression).\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:experimental_setup}\n\nWe evaluate ten configurations spanning six architecture families (1B--14B; Table~\\ref{tab:setup}), each generating $K{=}5$ depth levels from 5{,}000 C4~\\cite{raffel2020t5} seeds (256 tokens; 30{,}000 documents per configuration).\nDefault decoding: nucleus $p{=}0.95$, $T{=}1.0$; ablation covers greedy, $T{=}0.7$, and $T{=}1.2$.\nContinuation mode truncates seeds to 128 tokens and extends to 256; rewrite mode paraphrases the full text.\nEvaluation: 5-fold GroupKFold cross-validation grouped by seed document; pairwise $\\auc$ as primary metric.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Experimental configurations for depth-chain generation. \\textbf{Config}: shorthand identifier; \\textbf{Model}: architecture and parameter count; \\textbf{Mode}: generation strategy (continuation or rewrite); $N$: number of seed documents per depth level.}\n\\label{tab:setup}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{llcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Config} & \\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Mode} & $N$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen-rw   & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Rewrite & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-cont & Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-base & Qwen2.5-1.5B          & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nPythia    & Pythia-1.4B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nOLMo      & OLMo-1B               & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nGemma     & Gemma-2-2B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-7B   & Qwen2.5-7B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nQwen-14B  & Qwen2.5-14B           & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nLLaMA-8B  & LLaMA-3.1-8B          & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\nMistral   & Mistral-7B            & Continuation & 5{,}000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n",
      "numLines": 75,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 75
    }
  },

Starting with Batch 1 now. First up is rewriting that opening paragraph in the introduction.

The current paragraph establishes that synthetic content is contaminating web-scale corpora and that when this synthetic text gets fed back recursively, it degrades across generations—a phenomenon documented in the model collapse literature. The distinction between retrain-chains and prompt-chains is important because the authors focus on prompt-chains to isolate the text degradation from training dynamics. The key insight is that practitioners need to know at what generational depth the degradation becomes undetectable, yet current approaches treat all machine-generated text the same way without considering this generational dimension.

Now I need to sharpen this by distinguishing the two mechanisms more clearly: prompt-chains involve fixed models where text statistics decay with each generation, while retrain-chains involve weight shifts from retraining on synthetic data. I should emphasize that prompt-chains are the practically relevant case for web curation since most online synthetic text is one-shot generated without retraining. I'll also need to add a forward reference to the retrain-chain results (K*=7) to give readers the complete picture of where degradation becomes undetectable across both mechanisms. I'm distinguishing between two contamination mechanisms: retrain-chains where models iteratively train on their own outputs, and prompt-chains where a fixed model recursively generates text. Since most synthetic content on the web comes from one-shot generation rather than retraining, I'm focusing on prompt-chains as the more realistic scenario, though retrain-chain experiments suggest the full degradation range is wider than what prompt-chains alone would show. The distinction between retrain-chains and prompt-chains matters because they degrade synthetic text through different mechanisms—one through iterative model retraining, the other through recursive generation by a fixed model. I'm focusing on prompt-chains since they represent the dominant contamination pathway in practice: most synthetic text online comes from single-pass LLM generation rather than repeated retraining cycles. The retrain-chain experiments suggest the discrimination boundary sits higher at K=7, which means my prompt-chain findings establish a lower bound for what's detectable in real-world contamination scenarios.

For practitioners actually curating web data, the practical question shifts from whether degradation happens to how many generations deep it remains identifiable. The current approach treats all machine-generated text as equivalent and overlooks this generational depth dimension entirely.

Now I'm mapping out the edits needed across the paper—starting with the introduction rewrite, expanding the LOFO methodology section, then addressing generalizability claims throughout the abstract and experiments. I'll need to add limitations, systematize the K* notation, refine the practical recommendations, update the section titles with appropriate caveats, and clean up orphaned references.
